The complete drawing is reproduced using paths at the observed relative positions. |
| 148_scan009 | Canceled/reworked argument in the first Eell formula | \deleted{(\unsure{S})} | \note{dans la première formule, un argument biffé après $\mathbb{E}\mathrm{l}_1$} | \unsure{\deleted{(S))}&#124;\deleted{(S)}} | The dense cancellation crosses the top of the opening parenthesis and an S-shaped body. A further downturned inner stroke stands to the right of that body, followed by a larger outer closing parenthesis. The extra inner stroke could be an additional parenthesis-like trace or part of the reworking. The broad uncertainty covers both character identity and the extent of cancellation, since the parentheses are incompletely crossed. |
| 148_scan009 | Group attached below the wedge in the second Eell formula | \mathbin{\wedge}_{\mathbb Z/6\mathbb Z}S | \wedge^{\mathbf{Z}/6\mathbf{Z}} S | \mathbin{\wedge}_{\mathbb Z/6\mathbb Z}S | The pointed wedge is on the Eell baseline. Z/6Z is written below and to its right, with the following S back on the upper baseline. |
| 148_scan011 | line 5, generator formula | \unsure{\simeq}\,-\bar\zeta\,\unsure{j&#124;\zeta}=\exp\frac{2\pi}{6} | = -\bar{\zeta}$, $\zeta = \exp\frac{2\pi}{6} | \unsure{\simeq&#124;=}\,-\bar\zeta\,\unsure{j&#124;\zeta}=\exp\frac{2\pi}{6} | The initial relation has two upper obliques and a dark low loop/stroke, retaining ≃/= uncertainty. Minus and a separately barred zigzag ζ with a rounded low tail are clear. The next glyph has connected loops and long retraced descenders, leaving j/ζ open; no independent comma between the glyphs is secure. Equals, exp, 2π over 6, closing parenthesis and comma are visible. No i or separate opening parenthesis follows exp. |
| 148_scan011 | line 14, barycentre expression | (1-\bar\zeta)/3 | (1-\zeta)/3 | (1-\bar\zeta)/3 | A detached nearly horizontal bar stands above the zigzag ζ. Parentheses, 1, minus, slash and 3 are visible. |
| 148_scan015 | line 5, overwritten edge count | \deleted{\unsure{8&#124;6}} \inserted{\unsure{12}} | $12$ | \deleted{\unsure{8&#124;6}}\inserted{12} | The live 1 has a long ascending entry and a tall descending stroke; the separate 2 has a curved top, descending diagonal and horizontal foot. Thick vertical cancelling/retracing strokes cover the earlier digit. Its lower left bowl survives, while the upper part does not separate 8 from 6. The live 12 is resolved; the old digit is retained as doubtful. H acknowledges the surcharge in its drawing note but its running text omits the cancelled layer. |

The top-left slant stops before the top horizontal; both outer strokes at the left middle vertex and the left horizontal are separated. That horizontal stops before the lower central ring. The bottom horizontal begins far to the right of the bottom-left vertex. The lower-right radial strokes run in parallel and end separately, and the lower-right outer slant has detached ends. The upper-right perimeter is repeatedly retraced with parallel strokes, loops and gaps. Heavy middle horizontals have tapered ends, bulbs and a downward hook, not uniformly identifiable arrowheads. A separate oblique with a head-like tip lies below the left horizontal; small looped strokes lie over the upper horizontal and beside the lower-left diagonal. The draft reproduces these actual paths and varying weights without imposing C’s closed perimeter or solid upper point fills. |

### Unresolved places

- main D-star formula: `\unsure{\text{---}|\longrightarrow}` — headless stroke / right arrow
- cancelled word after qui: `\deleted{\unsure{suit|vient}}` — suit / vient
- margin unsubscripted Pi formula: `\unsure{\text{---}|\longrightarrow}` — headless stroke / right arrow
- margin projection phrase: `\unsure{des projections locales}` — compressed letters remain; fibrations excluded from final alternatives by p/j-like strokes
- margin newly recovered cancelled row: `\deleted{\illegible{2}}` — two words whose middle letters are obscured; no contextual completion
- margin et les Pi_d comme: `\uline{et} les \deleted{$\unsure{\Pi_d|P_d}$} \unsure{comme}` — cancellation extent on comme and overlap of les with Pi remain open; underline on et is clear
- upper cancelled marginal formula: `\deleted{$\unsure{\Pi_d|P_d}\longrightarrow\unsure{G_d|\Pi_d}$}` — Pi_d/P_d and G_d/Pi_d; overlapping strokes prevent resolution
- lowest cancelled marginal formula: `\deleted{$\unsure{D_d}\longrightarrow\unsure{D^*_{\Delta_r}}$}` — source-object placement and target remain uncertain under cancellation
- main Pi formula in finite-cover phrase: `\unsure{\text{---}|\longrightarrow}` — headless stroke / right arrow
- margin, sur below underlined et: `sur` — The word is clear; its attachment to the adjacent cancelled formula is unresolved.

- left note, adjective ending: `\unsure{décroissant|décroissants}` — décroissant; décroissants
- M0024, last interlinear insertion: `\unsure{ainsi|et}` — ainsi; et

### Unresolved places

- (226), three reworked superscripts: `\unsure{d|\delta}; \unsure{d|\delta}; \unsure{d+1|\delta+1|d1}` — d/delta forms and the final plus/1 scope remain open. The tall top-return strokes overlie lower looped bodies; no earlier form is supplied.
- caption word and quotations: `\unsure{``privé''|purifié|ponctué}` — The proposed first word privé follows the low v-like turn; purifié and ponctué remain inherited possibilities. Upper paired marks resemble quotation marks, but both their identities and the placement of the closing mark remain open.
- under induit: `\unsure{\deleted{est}}\inserted{induit}` — The low crossed loop and tall terminal loop support an est-like earlier word. The outer doubt covers both that reading and its deletion state: an elaborate looping underline remains possible.
- object deleted after sur: `\deleted{$\unsure{D|\Theta}_{d'_*}$}` — D or Theta under hatching. The d-prime-star subscript is readable.
- upper star in deleted Theta identification: `\unsure{*|\deleted{*}}` — A separately reworked star may be cancelled, but the layers cannot be separated; the entire baseline identification is subsequently crossed.
- separate upper run above s’identifie: `\inserted{\unsure{à}}` — Tentative à. Placement after s’identifie records a proposed attachment; its physical location is above the early part of the word, and its own run is uncrossed.
- punctuation between deleted D objects: `\unsure{:}` — One upper dot is visible; the possible lower colon dot is under the cancellation. An isolated dot remains possible.
- deleted where-word after D-tilde: `\unsure{où}` — The accent-like upper stroke survives, while the two baseline letters are covered by the horizontal cancellation.
- prior last digit under 227: `\deleted{\unsure{6}}` — The overwritten old digit remains unidentified. A 6-like loop is the tentative first reading only; other digit readings are open. The final 7 and first two 2 forms are read as written.
- trailing hook after cancelled d-prime: `\deleted{$d'$\unsure{,}}` — The d and prime are readable. The lower hook may be a comma, a pen return, or an incomplete subscript; no completed index is supplied.
- (229), reworked star on lower Theta: `\unsure{*|\deleted{*}}` — Star is heavily retraced; an independent cancellation is possible. No removal based on nearby formulas is made.
- last added word and its attachment: `\inserted{\unsure{est|du}}` — Est-like is favored over du; the t-like upright does not certify all preceding letters. The curved attachment surrounds d-prime, and the precise insertion point remains open.
- last d-prime cancellation scope: `\unsure{\deleted{d'}|d'}un` — The curve crosses/wraps the d-prime area and can be read as a local cancellation or an insertion leader. Only that part is doubtful; un is visibly uncrossed.

### Unresolved places

- before pour: `\unsure{vu}` — The V-shaped/looped crossed sign may be a word or sign; no further reading is established. Pour itself is clear.
- before l'image: `\unsure{:|à}` — Colon-like two-mark shape first; à remains open. The lower mark has no secure closed a loop.
- quotient-map terminal: `\unsure{\longrightarrow}` — Uncertainty attaches to the head's presence/form at the right end only. A small one-sided downward nub is visible; a full arrowhead is not secure. No established harpoon notation is asserted.
- initial strokes of inverse: `inverse` — Initial looped/retraced strokes overlap. The final word is readable; a separate underlying word or cancellation is not established.
- decoration over X after En termes de: `\unsure{\overline X|\vec X}` — Horizontal bar first; possible arrow decoration. The right end turns down without a secure converging head.
- first U^I, after torseur sous: `\unsure{\mathbb U^I}` — U body and doubled left stems match the later occurrence, but a horizontal crossing/reworking remains. Underlying letter(s), cancellation status and order of strokes are unresolved.
- after comme: `\unsure{une classe}` — Very low groups; the second initial is c-like and the remaining humps are compressed. Une classe remains a tentative reading.
- opening bracket after second U^I: `\bigl[` — An inner curved/vertical trace overlaps the clearer outer square bracket. No separate recoverable underlying character or chronological order is established.
- ending after déduit: `déduit\unsure{s}` — Final s versus the t's terminal return; H's e reading is also possible but less supported by the hooked end. Clear prefix déduit is retained.
- before structures: `\unsure{ses|les}` — Tall l-like loop and low s-like loop overlap. Initial letter and layer order remain open.
- closing delimiter after suppl.: `\unsure{\}|]}` — Inner brace-shaped trace and outer square-bracket trace; final form and chronological layer order remain open.

### Unresolved places

- line 4 upper insertion: `\unsure{$\mathbb Z/n_i$|$\mathbb Z_{n_i}$}` — A separate slash before n_i is preferred; its possible function as an initial stroke of a subscript n remains open. The group letter is a doubled Z.
- line 4 cancelled words: `\deleted{\unsure{de groupe|les groupes}}` — de groupe preferred from the looped initial; les groupes remains possible under the crossing rule. Cancellation itself is clear.
- line 5 cancelled sign after s: `\deleted{\uline{s}\unsure{\in}}` — The curved sign after the underlined s is read provisionally as in; crossing strokes obscure its interior bar. Cancellation of the local run is clear.
- line 7 proper-name accent: `Teichm\unsure{ü|u}ller` — The short mark over u joins a fine rising entry stroke into the first l. An umlaut represented by that short mark and an unaccented u with a raised l-entry remain possible.
- line 8 S subscript: `\unsure{*|1}` — The lower run is tangled or reworked; star and 1 are retained as witness candidates, with no clean single form certified and no separate strike layer assigned.
- line 8 X argument: `\unsure{X}` — X is the candidate reading of the main crossing diagonals. The additional loop and lower angular traces do not certify an earlier character or layer order.
- line 13 product ending: `\unsure{\mathbb Z_i;|\mathbb Z_{i_i}}` — First reading: Z_i and semicolon. Alternative: two i-like bodies in a repeated/stacked index. Two dots and two short lower bodies are visible, but their grouping is open.

### Unresolved places

- combinatoires initial run: `\unsure{com}binatoires` — The com junctions are overwritten; no independent earlier word or deletion layer can be read.
- marginal anneau: `\unsure{ann}eau` — Initial ann remains obscured by retracing; eau is readable. The opening parenthesis remains unmatched.
- upper drawing centre label: `\unsure{0|o}` — Round loop with rightward return; numeral zero and lower-case o are not separated by the ink.
- upper drawing below -i: `curved hook and detached dot reproduced as figure strokes` — Possible rotated question-like mark or another local figure mark; identity and attachment are not established. The actual two parts are retained.
- de centre round sign: `\unsure{0|o}` — Numeric 0 or lower-case o; no distinguishing stroke.
- vectoriel via round sign: `\unsure{0|o}` — Numeric 0 or lower-case o; the small closed loop does not decide.
- crossed axes: `\deleted{\unsure{axes} de}` — The strike masks the interiors of axes. de is plain and the whole axes de run is cancelled.
- punctuation after mod. signes: `\inserted{mod. signes\unsure{,}}` — The detached descending stroke is comma-shaped and separate from the leader; its attachment to the inserted phrase remains open.
- crossed word below réseau: `\deleted{\unsure{lattice}}` — lattice is the retained first reading; the tail under the strike does not allow every letter to be certified. No exact competing word is supplied.
- lower framed formula head and grouping: `\mathbb E\unsure{\ell_0(S)\,2|\ell_0(S)/2}` — The tall ell-like form has dense reworking. Its left curve may be part of ell rather than a parenthesis; the following letter/grouping remains open. The last descending curve may close the group or be a slash preceding the 2-shaped glyph. No prior layer can be recovered securely.
- lower framed formula index under Γ: `\Gamma_{\unsure{r|\Gamma|S}}` — The index is an upright curved-left stem with flat upper return; r, a small Γ, or an unfinished S remain possible. It is not silently equated with the clear Γ_S in the preceding prose.
- two loose dashes above lower grid: `two separate strokes reproduced at the top of the lower drawing` — They lie below coordonnées and above the top grid row. Their physical position is preserved; attachment to the word or to the drawing remains open.
- low dot pair after élement: `\raisebox{-.6em}{\unsure{:}}` — Two dark marks slant down-left well below the word baseline. A lowered doubtful colon preserves their presence; punctuation identity versus incidental ink, and attachment, remain open.
- Aut argument and closing parenthesis: `\operatorname{Aut}(\unsure{X_S|X})` — X is clear; the S-like lower trace and retraced closing parenthesis merge. There may be a subscript S or only a rewritten parenthesis.
- final peut initial letters: `\unsure{peu}t` — The initial run is densely reworked. The p descender and final t are present, but no prior letter sequence can be certified.
- final défini: `\unsure{défini}` — A tall f/descender and joined following run are visible; overwriting obscures the first e/accent junction and following letter boundaries. No earlier wording or deletion order is supplied.

### Unresolved places

- Cancelled start before La surface: `\unsure{$X$|$X_S$}` — X alone versus X with a lower S-shaped trace.
- Upper insertion after cell.: `\unsure{can|ces}` — can or ces, without a supplied final stop.
- Cancelled word after NB: `\unsure{le}` — The l loop is clear; the following letter remains hidden by the cancellation.
- Higher mark beside inserted 4: `\smallfrown` — A small arch is visible; its function and possible incomplete-circle form are unassigned.
- Square, crossed label below/right of center: `\eta` — Eta-like letter under crossing strokes; the exact underlying letter remains uncertain.
- Adjective before pour la description: `\unsure{adaptée|adapté}` — One or two terminal e letters after the tall t.
- Cancelled quotient, initial glyph: `\unsure{X}` — X-like crossed glyph with a curled lower trace, not fully recoverable.
- Cancelled quotient, loop after E: `\unsure{\ell_0|\ell}` — Ell with a lower zero versus the ell stroke alone.
- Cancelled quotient, parenthesized glyph: `\unsure{S|8}` — S or 8 under the diagonal cancellation.
- Cancelled sphere, upper-left label: `\unsure{\eta'^\natural}` — Prime and raised decoration obscured by crossing strokes.
- Cancelled sphere, lower-right label: `\unsure{\eta''^\natural}` — The high strokes remain inseparable under the cancellation.

- Under réseau: `\deleted{\unsure{lattice}}` — lattice is supported by l, two tall strokes and the i dot; the low a/ce portions remain crossed.
